we can finish the proof of this corollary by using (
2.93
).
∎

Finally, we establish the vanishing property under the Hölder regularity of the transmission eigenfunctions, which shall be useful for our study of the inverse elastic problem in Section
4
.

Theorem 2.3
.

Let
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})\in H^{1}(\Omega)^{2}\times H^{1}(\Omega)^{2}
be a pair of eigenfunctions to (
1.2
) associated with
\omega\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Let
W,S_{h}
and
q
be those described in Theorem
2.1
. Suppose that
\mathbf{v}\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S_{h}})^{2}
,
q\mathbf{w}\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S_{h}})^{2}
,
\eta\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}})
with
\eta(\mathbf{0})\neq 0
, for
\alpha\in(0,1)
and the corner
W
is non-degenerate. Then we have

(2.94)

\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{0})=\mathbf{0}.

Proof.

It is sufficient for us to show that (
2.94
) holds for
\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}
. Similar to (
2.27
), one can establish the following integral identity

(2.95)

\displaystyle-\omega^{2}\int_{S_{h}}(\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x})-\mathbf{f}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x}))\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}=I_{\Lambda_{h}}-\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}}\eta\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}{\rm d}\sigma,

where
I_{\Lambda_{h}}
is defined in (
2.27
) and
\mathbf{f}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x})
is given in (
2.41
).

Since
\mathbf{f}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0})\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S}_{h})
and
\eta\in C^{\alpha}\left(\overline{\Gamma}_{h}^{\pm}\right)
, we know that
\eta
and
\mathbf{f}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x})
have the expansions (
2.39
) and (
2.41
) around the origin, respectively. Furthermore, due to the fact that
\mathbf{v}\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S}_{h})^{2}
, we have the following expansions

(2.96)

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0})+\delta\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x}),\quad|\delta\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}|\leq\|\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x})\|_{C^{\alpha}(\Omega)^{2}}|\mathbf{x}|^{\alpha}.

Substituting (
2.39
), (
2.41
) and (
2.96
) into (
2.95
), we can derive that

(2.97)

\displaystyle-\omega^{2}(\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0})-\mathbf{f}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0}))\int_{S_{h}}\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}-\omega^{2}\int_{S_{h}}(\delta\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x})-\delta\mathbf{f}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x}))\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle=I_{\Lambda_{h}}-\eta(\mathbf{0})\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0})\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}}\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\sigma-\eta(\mathbf{0})\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}}\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x})\cdot\delta\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\sigma

\displaystyle-\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0})\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}}\delta\eta(\mathbf{x})\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\sigma-\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}}\delta\eta(\mathbf{x})\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x})\cdot\delta\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\sigma.

From (
2.97
), after rearranging terms, we have

(2.98)

\displaystyle\eta(\mathbf{0})\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0})\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}}\mathbf{u}{\rm d}\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\omega^{2}(\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0})-\mathbf{f}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0}))\int_{S_{h}}\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}-\int_{S_{h}}\omega^{2}(\delta\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x})-\delta\mathbf{f}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x}))\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle+I_{\Lambda_{h}}-\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0})\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}}\delta\eta(\mathbf{x})\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\sigma-\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}}\delta\eta(\mathbf{x})\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x})\cdot\delta\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\sigma

\displaystyle-\eta(\mathbf{0})\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}}\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x})\cdot\delta\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\sigma.

Using (
2.49
) in Lemma
2.11
, we have

(2.99)

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{-}}\mathbf{u}{\rm d}\sigma

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
{\rm i}\end{pmatrix}\int_{0}^{h}e^{-s\sqrt{r}\mu(\theta_{m})}{\rm d}r

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
{\rm i}\end{pmatrix}2s^{-2}\bigg(\mu(\theta_{m})^{-2}-\mu(\theta_{m})^{-2}e^{-s\sqrt{h}\mu(\theta_{m})}-\mu(\theta_{m})^{-1}s\sqrt{h}e^{-s\sqrt{h}\mu(\theta_{m})}\bigg),

and

(2.100)

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{+}}\mathbf{u}{\rm d}\sigma

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
{\rm i}\end{pmatrix}\int_{0}^{h}e^{-s\sqrt{r}\mu(\theta_{M})}{\rm d}r

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}1\\
{\rm i}\end{pmatrix}2s^{-2}\bigg(\mu(\theta_{M})^{-2}-\mu(\theta_{M})^{-2}e^{-s\sqrt{h}\mu(\theta_{M})}-\mu(\theta_{M})^{-1}s\sqrt{h}e^{-s\sqrt{h}\mu(\theta_{M})}\bigg),

where
\mu(\theta)
is defined in (
2.49
).
By virtue of (
2.48
), using (
2.39
) and (
2.96
) we have the estimates

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{-}}\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x})\cdot\delta\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\sigma\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\|\mathbf{v}^{\sf{R}}\|_{C^{\alpha}}\int_{0}^{h}r^{\alpha}e^{-s\sqrt{r}\cos\frac{\theta_{m}}{2}}{\rm d}r=O(s^{-2\alpha-2}),

(2.101)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{-}}\delta\eta(\mathbf{x})\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\sigma\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\int_{0}^{h}r^{\alpha}e^{-s\sqrt{r}\cos\frac{\theta_{m}}{2}}{\rm d}r=O(s^{-2\alpha-2}),

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{-}}\delta\eta(\mathbf{x})\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x})\cdot\delta\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\sigma\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\|\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}\|_{C^{\alpha}}\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\int_{0}^{h}r^{2\alpha}e^{-s\sqrt{r}\cos\frac{\theta_{m}}{2}}{\rm d}r=O(s^{-4\alpha-2}),

and

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{+}}\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x})\cdot\delta\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\sigma\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\|\mathbf{v}^{\sf{R}}\|_{C^{\alpha}}\int_{0}^{h}r^{\alpha}e^{-s\sqrt{r}\cos\frac{\theta_{M}}{2}}{\rm d}r=O(s^{-2\alpha-2}),

(2.102)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{+}}\delta\eta(\mathbf{x})\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\sigma\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\int_{0}^{h}r^{\alpha}e^{-s\sqrt{r}\cos\frac{\theta_{M}}{2}}{\rm d}r=O(s^{-2\alpha-2}),

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{+}}\delta\eta(\mathbf{x})\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x})\cdot\delta\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\sigma\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}\|\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}\|_{C^{\alpha}}\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\int_{0}^{h}r^{2\alpha}e^{-s\sqrt{r}\cos\frac{\theta_{M}}{2}}{\rm d}r=O(s^{-4\alpha-2}),

as
s\rightarrow+\infty
.

Similarly, using (
2.18
), (
2.41
) and (
2.96
), one has

(2.103)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{S_{h}}\delta\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\|\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}\|_{C^{\alpha}}\int_{W}|u_{1}(\mathbf{x})||\mathbf{x}|^{\alpha}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}\|\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}\|_{C^{\alpha}}\frac{(\theta_{M}-\theta_{m})\Gamma(2\alpha+4)}{\delta_{W}^{2\alpha+4}}s^{-2\alpha-4},

\displaystyle\left|\int_{S_{h}}\delta\mathbf{f}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}\|\mathbf{f}_{\sf{R}}\|_{C^{\alpha}}\int_{W}|u_{1}(\mathbf{x})||\mathbf{x}|^{\alpha}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\sqrt{2}\|\mathbf{f}_{\sf{R}}\|_{C^{\alpha}}\frac{(\theta_{M}-\theta_{m})\Gamma(2\alpha+4)}{\delta_{W}^{2\alpha+4}}s^{-2\alpha-4},

as
s\rightarrow+\infty
.

Multiplying
s^{2}
on the both side of (
2.98
), by virtue of (
2.33
), (
2.99
), (
2.100
), (
2.101
), (
2
), and (
2.103
), and letting
s\rightarrow+\infty
, we have

(2.104)

\displaystyle\eta(\mathbf{0})\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0})\cdot\begin{pmatrix}1\\
{\rm i}\end{pmatrix}\bigg(\mu(\theta_{M})^{-2}+\mu(\theta_{m})^{-2}\bigg)=0.

Since the corner
W
is non-degenerate, namely
\theta_{M}-\theta_{m}\neq\pi
, from
[
26
, Lemma 2.10]
, we know that

\mu^{-2}(\theta_{M})+\mu^{-2}(\theta_{m})\neq 0.

Finally, by noting
\eta(\mathbf{0})\neq\mathbf{0}
, it is easy to infer from (
2.104
) that
\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0})=\mathbf{0}
.

The proof is complete.
∎

Remark 2.3
.

Similar to Corollary
2.2
, under the same setup of Theorem
2.3
, for the degenerate case of (
1.2
) with
\eta\equiv 0
in (
1.2
), if
V
is Hölder-regular near the corner and
V(\mathbf{0})\neq 0
, one can prove that
\mathbf{v}(\mathbf{0})=\mathbf{0}
, where
\mathbf{v}\in H^{1}(\Omega)^{2}\cap C^{\alpha}(\overline{S_{h}})
and
\mathbf{w}\in H^{1}(\Omega)^{2}
are a pair of the generalized elastic transmssion eigenfunction to (
1.2
) associated with
\omega\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
such that
q\mathbf{w}\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S_{h}})
. We choose not to discuss the details in this paper. In
[
8
, Theorem 1.5]
, the corresponding vanishing property was established when
\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{w}\in H^{2}(\Omega)^{2}
,
V
is Hölder continuous around the corner such that
V(\mathbf{0})\neq 0
, and either
\mathbf{v}
or
\mathbf{w}
is Hölder continuous around the corner. Compared with
[
8
, Theorem 1.5]
, the assumption
\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{w}\in H^{2}(\Omega)^{2}
can be removed in our setting.

3.
vanishing near corners of generalized elastic transmission eigenfunctions: three-dimensional case

In this section, we establish the vanishing property of the generalized elastic transmission eigenfunctions for the 3D case. In principle, we could also consider a generic corner in the usual sense as the one for the 2D case. However, we consider a 3D corner described by
S_{h}\times(-M,M)
, where
S_{h}
is defined in (
2.2
) and
M\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. The 3D corner
S_{h}\times(-M,M)
is a more general corner geometry in 3D. It is readily seen that
S_{h}\times(-M,M)
actually describes an edge singularity. In what follows, we suppose that the Lipschitz domain
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{3}
with
\mathbf{0}\in\partial\Omega
possesses a 3D corner. Let
\mathbf{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
be the vertex of
S_{h}
and
x_{3}\in(-M,M)
. Then
(\mathbf{0},x_{3})
is defined as an edge point of
S_{h}\times(-M,M)
.

In order to make use of the CGO solution
\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x})
introduced in Lemma
2.2
to study the vanishing property of
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
to (
1.2
) at a 3D corner, we define the following dimension reduction operator.

Definition 3.1
.

Let
{S_{h}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
be defined in (
2.2
),
M>0
. For a given function
\mathbf{g}
in the domain
S_{h}\times(-M,M)
. Pick up any point
x_{3}\in(-M,M)
. Suppose that
\phi\in C_{0}^{\infty}((x_{3}-L,x_{3}+L))
is a nonnegative function and
\phi\not\equiv 0
, where
L
is sufficiently small such that
(x_{3}-L,x_{3}+L)\subset(-M,M)
, and write
\mathbf{x}=(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3},\,\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. The dimension reduction operator
\mathcal{R}
is defined by

(3.1)

\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{g})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\int_{{x}_{3}-L}^{{x}_{3}+L}\phi(x_{3})\mathbf{g}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3},

where
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in{S}_{h}
.

Before presenting the main results of this section, we first analyze the regularity of the functions after applying the dimension reduction operator. Using a similar argument of
[
6
, Lemma 3.4]
, we can prove the following lemma, whose detailed proof is omitted.

Lemma 3.1
.

Let
\mathbf{g}\in H^{m}({S}_{h}\times(-M,M))^{3}
,
m=1,2
. Then

{\mathcal{R}}(\mathbf{g})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\in H^{m}({S}_{h})^{3}.

Similarly, if
\mathbf{g}\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S_{h}}\times[-M,M])^{3}
,
0<\alpha<1
, then

{\mathcal{R}}(\mathbf{g})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S_{h}})^{3}.

The elastic Herglotz wave function
\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{g}}
in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
is defined by

(3.2)

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{\mathbf{g}}(\mathbf{x})=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\{e^{\mathrm{i}k_{p}\mathbf{x}\cdot\mathbf{d}}\mathbf{g}_{p}(\mathbf{d})+e^{\mathrm{i}k_{s}\mathbf{x}\cdot\mathbf{d}}\mathbf{g}_{s}(\mathbf{d})\}{\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d}),

where the kernel
\mathbf{g}=\mathbf{g}_{p}+\mathbf{g}_{s}
with
\mathbf{g}_{p}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{3}
and
\mathbf{g}_{s}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{3}
and
{\mathbf{d}}\in\mathbb{S}^{2}
. Lemma
2.1
holds equally in the three dimensions. In view of this lemma, for any pair of the generalized elastic transmission eigenfunction
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
to (
1.2
), there exits a sequence Herglotz wave function
\{\mathbf{v}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{+\infty}
defined by

(3.3)

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{j}(\mathbf{x})=\int_{S^{2}}\{e^{ik_{p}\mathbf{x}\cdot\mathbf{d}}\mathbf{g}_{jp}(\mathbf{d})+e^{ik_{s}\mathbf{x}\cdot\mathbf{d}}\mathbf{g}_{js}(\mathbf{d})\}{\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d}),

where
\mathbf{g}_{j}=\mathbf{g}_{jp}+\mathbf{g}_{js}
with
\mathbf{g}_{jp}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{3}
and
\mathbf{g}_{js}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{3}
, can approximate
\mathbf{v}
to an arbitrary accuracy in
H^{1}(\Omega)^{3}
. Henceforth, we let the real and imaginary parts of the kernel functions
\mathbf{g}_{j\beta}({\mathbf{d}})
(
\beta=p,s
) in (
3.3
) be defined by

(3.4)

\mathbf{g}_{j\beta}({\mathbf{d}})=\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},j\beta}({\mathbf{d}})+\mathrm{i}\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},j\beta}({\mathbf{d}}),

where

(3.5)

\displaystyle\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},jp}({\mathbf{d}})=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},jp}^{(1,2)}({\mathbf{d}})\\
g_{{\sf{R}},jp}^{(3)}({\mathbf{d}})\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{3},\ \mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},js}({\mathbf{d}})=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},js}^{(1,2)}({\mathbf{d}})\\
g_{{\sf{R}},js}^{(3)}({\mathbf{d}})\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{3},

\displaystyle\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},jp}({\mathbf{d}})=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},jp}^{(1,2)}({\mathbf{d}})\\
g_{{\sf{I}},jp}^{(3)}({\mathbf{d}})\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{3},\ \mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},js}({\mathbf{d}})=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},js}^{(1,2)}({\mathbf{d}})\\
g_{{\sf{I}},js}^{(3)}({\mathbf{d}})\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{3}

with
\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},j\beta}^{(1,2)}({\mathbf{d}})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},j\beta}^{(1,2)}({\mathbf{d}})\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Similar to Proposition
2.2
, using Jacobi-Anger expansion we have the expansion of the real and imaginary part of
\mathbf{v}_{j}(\mathbf{x})
defined in (
3.3
) as follows.

Proposition 3.1
.

Let the elastic Herglotz wave function
\mathbf{v}_{j}
be defined by (
3.3
). Denote

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{jp}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0})=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{jp}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{d}){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d}),\ \mathbf{v}_{js}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0})=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{js}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{d}){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{jp}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{0})=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{jp}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{d}){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d}),\ \mathbf{v}_{js}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{0})=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{js}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{d}){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d}).

Then

(3.6)

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{j}(\mathbf{0})

\displaystyle=\mathbf{v}_{j}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0})+{\rm i}\mathbf{v}_{j}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{0})=(\mathbf{v}_{jp}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0})+\mathbf{v}_{js}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{0}))+{\rm i}(\mathbf{v}_{jp}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{0})+\mathbf{v}_{js}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{0})).

where

g_{j\beta}^{{\sf{R}}}({\mathbf{d}})
and
g_{j\beta}^{{\sf{I}}}({\mathbf{d}})
(
\beta=p,s
) are defined in (
3.4
). Let
j_{\ell}(t)
be the
\ell
-th Bessel function for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
.
Furthermore, we have

(3.7)

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{j}(\mathbf{x})=\mathbf{v}_{j}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x})+\rm i\mathbf{v}_{j}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{x}),

where

(3.8)

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{j}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle j_{0}(k_{p}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{jp}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{d}){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d})+j_{0}(k_{s}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{js}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{d}){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d})

\displaystyle+\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell+1)j_{2\ell}(k_{p}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{jp}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{d})P_{2\ell}(\cos\varphi){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d})

\displaystyle+\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell+1)j_{2\ell}(k_{s}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{js}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{d})P_{2\ell}(\cos\varphi){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d})

\displaystyle+\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell-1)j_{2\ell-1}(k_{p}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{jp}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{d})P_{2\ell-1}(\cos\varphi){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d})

\displaystyle+\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell-1)j_{2\ell-1}(k_{s}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{js}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{d})P_{2\ell-1}(\cos\varphi){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{j}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle j_{0}(k_{p}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{jp}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{d}){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d})+j_{0}(k_{s}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{js}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{d}){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d})

\displaystyle-\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell+1)j_{2\ell}(k_{p}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{jp}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{d})P_{2\ell}(\cos\varphi){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d})

\displaystyle-\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell+1)j_{2\ell}(k_{s}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{js}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{d})P_{2\ell}(\cos\varphi){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d})

\displaystyle+\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell-1)j_{2\ell-1}(k_{p}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{jp}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{d})P_{2\ell-1}(\cos\varphi){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d})

\displaystyle+\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell-1)j_{2\ell-1}(k_{s}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{js}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{d})P_{2\ell-1}(\cos\varphi){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d}),

and
\varphi
is the angle between
\mathbf{x}
and
\mathbf{d}
in (
3.3
).

In view of the explicit expression
\mathbf{v}_{j}(\mathbf{x})
defined in (
3.3
), using Euler formula, one can handily compute that

Proposition 3.2
.

Let the elastic Herglotz wave function
\mathbf{v}_{j}
be defined in (
3.3
), where
\mathbf{v}_{j}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x})
and
\mathbf{v}_{j}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{x})
are the real and imaginary parts of
\mathbf{v}_{j}
respectively. Then it holds that

(3.9)

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{j}^{\sf{R}}(\mathbf{x})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\bigg(\cos(k_{p}\mathbf{d}\cdot\mathbf{x})\mathbf{g}_{jp}^{\sf{R}}({\mathbf{d}})-\sin(k_{p}\mathbf{d}\cdot\mathbf{x})\mathbf{g}_{jp}^{\sf{I}}({\mathbf{d}})+\cos(k_{s}\mathbf{d}\cdot\mathbf{x})\mathbf{g}_{js}^{\sf{R}}({\mathbf{d}})

\displaystyle-\sin(k_{s}\mathbf{d}\cdot\mathbf{x})\mathbf{g}_{js}^{\sf{I}}({\mathbf{d}})\bigg){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d}),

and

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{j}^{\sf{I}}(\mathbf{x})=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\bigg(\cos(k_{p}\mathbf{d}\cdot\mathbf{x})\mathbf{g}_{jp}^{\sf{I}}({\mathbf{d}})+\sin(k_{p}\mathbf{d}\cdot\mathbf{x})\mathbf{g}_{jp}^{\sf{R}}({\mathbf{d}})+\cos(k_{s}\mathbf{d}\cdot\mathbf{x})\mathbf{g}_{js}^{\sf{I}}({\mathbf{d}})

\displaystyle+\sin(k_{s}\mathbf{d}\cdot\mathbf{x})\mathbf{g}_{js}^{\sf{R}}({\mathbf{d}})\bigg){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d}).

Let
{S_{h}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{2}
be defined in (
2.2
) and
M>0
. For any fixed
x_{3}\in(-M,M)
and
L>0
defined in Definition
3.1
, we suppose that
L
is sufficiently small such that
(x_{3}-L,x_{3}+L)\subset(-M,M)
. Write
\mathbf{x}=(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3})\in\mathbb{R}^{3},\,\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. In what follows, we consider the transmission eigenvalue problem for
\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}\in H^{1}({S}_{h}\times(-M,M))^{3}
:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\lambda\Delta{\mathbf{v}}+(\lambda+\mu)\nabla\nabla\cdot{\mathbf{v}}+\omega^{2}{\mathbf{v}}={\mathbf{0}}}\hskip 56.9055pt\ \mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in S_{h},\ -M<x_{3}<M,\\[5.0pt]
{\lambda\Delta{\mathbf{w}}+(\lambda+\mu)\nabla\nabla\cdot{\mathbf{w}}+\omega^{2}(1+V){\mathbf{w}}={\mathbf{0}}}\hskip 14.22636pt\ \mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in S_{h},\ -M<x_{3}<M,\\[5.0pt]
{\mathbf{w}}={\mathbf{v}},\quad T_{\nu}{\mathbf{v}}+\eta{\mathbf{v}}=T_{\nu}{\mathbf{w}}\hskip 91.04872pt\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\in\Gamma_{h}^{\pm},\ -M<x_{3}<M,\end{array}\right.

where
\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}
are defined in (
2.2
),
T_{\nu}
is boundary traction operator to
\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}\times(-M,M)
,
q\in L^{\infty}(S_{h}\times(-M,M))
defined in (
1.12
) and
\eta\in L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}\times(-M,M))
is independent of
x_{3}
. Similar to the 2D case, we let
(\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}},\mathbf{w}_{\sf{R}})
and
(\mathbf{v}_{\sf I},\mathbf{w}_{\sf I})
respectively signify the real and imaginary parts of
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
, and both of them satisfy the Lamé system (
3
). We shall mainly focus on dealing with
(\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}},\mathbf{w}_{\sf{R}})
and all the results hold equally for
(\mathbf{v}_{\sf I},\mathbf{w}_{\sf I})
, and hence
(\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w})
.

Noting that
\eta
in (
3
) is independent of the
x_{3}
variable, and by applying the reduction operator
\mathcal{R}
defined in Definition
3.1
, one can show by direct verifications the following lemma.

Lemma 3.2
.

Denote

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=

\displaystyle-\omega^{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})-\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime\prime}(x_{3})\begin{pmatrix}\lambda v_{1}\\
\lambda v_{2}\\
(2\lambda+\mu)v_{3}\end{pmatrix}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3}

\displaystyle+(\lambda+\mu)\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime}(x_{3})\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{1}v_{3}\\
\partial_{2}v_{3}\\
\partial_{1}v_{1}+\partial_{2}v_{2}\end{pmatrix}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3},

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}_{2}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=

\displaystyle-\omega^{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{f}_{\sf{R}})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})-\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime\prime}(x_{3})\begin{pmatrix}\lambda w_{1}\\
\lambda w_{2}\\
(2\lambda+\mu)w_{3}\end{pmatrix}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3}

\displaystyle+(\lambda+\mu)\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime}(x_{3})\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{1}w_{3}\\
\partial_{2}w_{3}\\
\partial_{1}w_{1}+\partial_{2}w_{2}\end{pmatrix}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3},

where
\mathbf{f}_{\sf{R}}:=(1+V)\mathbf{w}_{\sf{R}}=q\mathbf{w}_{\sf{R}}
and
\mathcal{R}
is the dimension reduction operator associated with
\phi
defined in Definition
3.1
. Denote

(3.13)

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}=\begin{pmatrix}v_{1}\\
v_{2}\\
v_{3}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}\\
v_{3}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{3},\quad\mathbf{w}_{\sf{R}}=\begin{pmatrix}w_{1}\\
w_{2}\\
w_{3}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{w}^{(1,2)}\\
w_{3}\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{3},

where
\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
and
\mathbf{w}^{(1,2)}\in\mathbb{R}^{2}
. Assume that
\eta\in L^{\infty}(\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}\times(-M,M))
in (
3
) is independent of
x_{3}
. Then it holds that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\mathbf{G}_{1}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}\hskip 213.39566pt\ \mbox{ in }S_{h},\\[5.0pt]
{\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{w}_{\sf{R}})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\mathbf{G}_{2}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}\hskip 213.39566pt\ \mbox{ in }S_{h},\\[5.0pt]
\mathcal{R}({\mathbf{w}}_{\sf{R}})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\mathcal{R}({\mathbf{v}}_{\sf{R}})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),\,\hskip 207.7052pt\mbox{on }\Gamma_{h}^{\pm},\\[5.0pt]
\begin{bmatrix}T_{\nu}\mathcal{R}({\mathbf{v}}^{(1,2)})+\lambda\mathcal{R}(\partial_{3}v_{3})\nu\\
\mu\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{R}(v_{3})+\mu\begin{bmatrix}\mathcal{R}(\partial_{3}v_{1})\\
\mathcal{R}(\partial_{3}v_{2})\end{bmatrix}\nu\end{bmatrix}+\eta\mathcal{R}({\mathbf{v}}_{\sf{R}})=\begin{bmatrix}T_{\nu}\mathcal{R}({\mathbf{w}}^{(1,2)})+\lambda\mathcal{R}(\partial_{3}w_{3})\nu\\
\mu\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{R}(w_{3})+\mu\begin{bmatrix}\mathcal{R}(\partial_{3}w_{1})\\
\mathcal{R}(\partial_{3}w_{2})\end{bmatrix}\nu\end{bmatrix}\mbox{on }\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}\end{array}\right.

in the distributional sense, where
\nu
signifies the exterior unit normal vector to
\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}
,
T_{\nu}
is the two dimensional boundary traction operator defined in (
1.12
) and

(3.18)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathcal{L}}=\begin{pmatrix}\lambda\Delta^{\prime}+(\lambda+\mu)\partial_{1}^{2}&(\lambda+\mu)\partial_{1}\partial_{2}&0\\
(\lambda+\mu)\partial_{1}\partial_{2}&\lambda\Delta^{\prime}+(\lambda+\mu)\partial_{2}^{2}&0\\
0&0&\lambda\Delta^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}:=\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{L}&0\\
0&\lambda\Delta^{\prime}\end{pmatrix}

with
\Delta^{\prime}:=\partial_{1}^{2}+\partial_{2}^{2}
being the Laplace operator with respect to the
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
-variables. Here
\mathcal{L}
is the two dimensional Lamé operator with respect to the
\mathbf{x}^{\prime}
-variable.

Lemma 3.3
.

Under the same setup in Lemma
3.2
, the PDE system (
3.2
) is equivalent to

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\mathbf{G}^{(1,2)}_{1}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}\hskip 130.88284pt\ \mbox{ in }S_{h},\\[5.0pt]
{\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{w}^{(1,2)})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\mathbf{G}^{(1,2)}_{2}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}\hskip 128.0374pt\ \mbox{ in }S_{h},\\[5.0pt]
{\mathcal{R}({\mathbf{w}}^{(1,2)})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\mathcal{R}({\mathbf{v}}^{(1,2)})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),\hskip 119.50148pt\mbox{ on }\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}},\\[5.0pt]
{\ T_{\nu}\mathcal{R}({\mathbf{v}}^{(1,2)})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})+\eta\mathcal{R}({\mathbf{v}}^{(1,2)})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=T_{\nu}\mathcal{R}({\mathbf{w}}^{(1,2)})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\hskip 17.07182pt\mbox{ on }\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}},\end{array}\right.

and

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}{\lambda\Delta^{\prime}\mathcal{R}(v_{3})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\mathbf{G}_{1}^{(3)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}\hskip 193.47882pt\ \mbox{ in }S_{h},\\[5.0pt]
{\lambda\Delta^{\prime}\mathcal{R}(w_{3})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\mathbf{G}_{2}^{(3)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})}\hskip 190.63338pt\ \mbox{ in }S_{h},\\[5.0pt]
\mathcal{R}(w_{3})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\mathcal{R}(v_{3})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{R}(v_{3})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})+\mu^{-1}\eta\mathcal{R}(v_{3})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\partial_{\nu}\mathcal{R}(w_{3})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\hskip 0.85355pt\mbox{on }\Gamma_{h}^{\pm},\end{array}\right.

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}_{1}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\omega^{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})-\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime\prime}(x_{3})\begin{pmatrix}\lambda v_{1}\\
\lambda v_{2}\end{pmatrix}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3}

\displaystyle+(\lambda+\mu)\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime}(x_{3})\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{1}v_{3}\\
\partial_{2}v_{3}\end{pmatrix}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3},

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}_{2}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\omega^{2}\mathcal{R}(q\mathbf{w}^{(1,2)})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})-\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime\prime}(x_{3})\begin{pmatrix}\lambda w_{1}\\
\lambda w_{2}\end{pmatrix}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3}

\displaystyle+(\lambda+\mu)\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime}(x_{3})\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{1}w_{3}\\
\partial_{2}w_{3}\end{pmatrix}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3},

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}_{1}^{(3)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\omega^{2}\mathcal{R}({v}_{3})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})-(2\lambda+\mu)\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime\prime}(x_{3})v_{3}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3}

\displaystyle+(\lambda+\mu)\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime}(x_{3})(\partial_{1}v_{1}+\partial_{2}v_{2})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3},

\displaystyle\mathbf{G}_{2}^{(3)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\omega^{2}\mathcal{R}(q{w}_{3})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})-(2\lambda+\mu)\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime\prime}(x_{3})w_{3}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3}

\displaystyle+(\lambda+\mu)\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime}(x_{3})(\partial_{1}w_{1}+\partial_{2}w_{2})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3}.

Proof.

Since
\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}=\mathbf{w}_{\sf{R}}
on
\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}\times[-L,L]
, using (
3.1
), it can be directly deduced that

(3.26)

\mathcal{R}(\partial_{3}(\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}-\mathbf{w}_{\sf{R}}))=-\int_{-L}^{L}\phi^{\prime}(x_{3})(\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}-\mathbf{w}_{\sf{R}})=\mathbf{0}\quad\mbox{ on }\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}.

From (
3.2
), by virtue of (
3.18
), (
3.13
) and (
3.26
), together with straightforward calculations, one can obtain (
3.3
) and (
3.3
) respectively.
∎

Next we mainly study the system (
3.3
).

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
S_{h}
,
\Lambda_{h}
and
\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}
be defined in (
2.2
). Suppose that
\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)},\mathbf{w}^{(1,2)}\in H^{1}({S}_{h}\times(-M,M))^{2}
fulfill (
3.3
). Recall that the CGO solution
\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x})
is defined in (
2.16
) and
P_{n}(t)
is the Legendre polynomial. Let
\beta=p\mbox{ or }s
,
j_{\ell,\beta}=j_{\ell}(k_{\beta}|\mathbf{x}|)
and

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{jp}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{0})=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},jp}^{(1,2)}({\mathbf{d}}){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d}),\ \mathbf{v}_{js}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{0})=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},js}^{(1,2)}({\mathbf{d}}){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d}),

\displaystyle\mathbf{B}_{j\beta,1}^{(\ell)}=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},j\beta}^{(1,2)}({\mathbf{d}})P_{2\ell}(\cos\varphi){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d}),\quad\mathbf{B}_{j\beta,2}^{(\ell)}=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},j\beta}^{(1,2)}({\mathbf{d}})P_{2\ell-1}(\cos\varphi){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d}).

Denote

\mathbf{v}_{j}^{{\sf{R}}}(\mathbf{x})=\begin{pmatrix}\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{x})\\
v_{j}^{(3)}(\mathbf{x})\end{pmatrix}\in\mathbb{R}^{3},\quad\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{x})\in\mathbb{R}^{2},

where

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}_{j}(\mathbf{x})=

\displaystyle\mathbf{v}_{jp}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{0})j_{0}(k_{p}|\mathbf{x}|)+\mathbf{v}_{js}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{0})j_{0}(k_{s}|\mathbf{x}|)+\sum_{\beta=p,\,s}\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell+1)j_{2\ell,\beta}\mathbf{B}_{j\beta,1}^{(\ell)}

(3.27)

\displaystyle+2\sum_{\beta=p,\,s}\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell-1)j_{2\ell-1,\beta}\mathbf{B}_{j\beta,2}^{(\ell)},

and

\displaystyle v_{j}^{(3)}(\mathbf{x})=

\displaystyle j_{0}(k_{p}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{{\sf{R}},jp}^{(3)}(\mathbf{d}){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d})+j_{0}(k_{s}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{{\sf{R}},js}^{(3)}(\mathbf{d}){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d})

\displaystyle+\sum_{\beta=p,\,s}\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell+1)j_{2\ell}(k_{\beta}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{{\sf{R}},j\beta}^{(3)}(\mathbf{d})P_{2\ell}(\cos\varphi){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d})

\displaystyle+\sum_{\beta=p,\,s}\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell-1)j_{2\ell-1}(k_{\beta}|\mathbf{x}|)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g_{{\sf{I}},j\beta}^{(3)}(\mathbf{d})P_{2\ell-1}(\cos\varphi){\rm d}\sigma(\mathbf{d})

with
\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},j\beta}^{(1,2)}
,
\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},j\beta}^{(1,2)}
,
g_{{\sf{R}},j\beta}^{(3)}
and
g_{{\sf{I}},j\beta}^{(3)}
defined in (
3.5
). Then the following integral equality holds

(3.28)

\widetilde{I}_{1}+\widetilde{I}_{2}=\widetilde{I}_{\Lambda_{h}}-\widetilde{I}_{\pm}-\widetilde{I}_{\pm}^{\Delta},

where

(3.29)

\displaystyle\widetilde{I}_{1}=\int_{S_{h}}(\mathbf{f}_{1j}+\mathbf{f}_{2}+\mathbf{f}_{3}+\mathbf{f}_{4})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime},\ \widetilde{I}_{2}=\int_{S_{h}}(\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{1j})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime},

\displaystyle\widetilde{I}_{\Lambda_{h}}=\int_{\Lambda_{h}}(T_{\nu}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{w}^{(1,2)}))\cdot\mathbf{u}-(T_{\nu}(\mathbf{u}))\cdot\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{w}^{(1,2)}){\rm d}\sigma,

\displaystyle\widetilde{I}_{\pm}=\int_{\Gamma^{\pm}_{h}}\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\sigma,\ \widetilde{I}_{\pm}^{\Delta}=\int_{\Gamma^{\pm}_{h}}\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\sigma,

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{1}=-\omega^{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}),\ \mathbf{f}_{2}=\omega^{2}\mathcal{R}(q\mathbf{w}^{(1,2)}),\ \mathbf{f}_{3}=-\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime\prime}(x_{3})\begin{pmatrix}\lambda(v_{1}-w_{1})\\
\lambda(v_{2}-w_{2})\end{pmatrix}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3},

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{1j}=-\omega^{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)}),\ \mathbf{f}_{4}=(\lambda+\mu)\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime}(x_{3})\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{1}(v_{3}-w_{3})\\
\partial_{2}(v_{3}-w_{3})\end{pmatrix}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3}.

Proof.

Recall that
\mathcal{L}
is defined in (
3.18
). Since
\mu>0
, we see that
3\lambda+2\mu>0
implies
3(\lambda+\mu)>0
and hence also
2\lambda+2\mu>0
, so
\mathcal{L}
is strongly elliptic. Using Green’s formula (
2.26
) on the domain
S_{h}
together with
{\mathcal{L}}\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{0}
in
S_{h}
, we have

(3.30)

\displaystyle\int_{S_{h}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{w}^{(1,2)})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma^{\pm}_{h}\cup\Lambda_{h}}(T_{\nu}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{w}^{(1,2)}))\cdot\mathbf{u}-(T_{\nu}(\mathbf{u}))\cdot\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{w}^{(1,2)}){\rm d}\sigma.

Using the boundary condition in (
3.3
), it yields that

(3.31)

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma^{\pm}_{h}}(T_{\nu}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{w}^{(1,2)}))\cdot\mathbf{u}-(T_{\nu}(\mathbf{u}))\cdot\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{w}^{(1,2)}){\rm d}\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma^{\pm}_{h}}\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\sigma

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{\Gamma^{\pm}_{h}}\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\sigma-\int_{\Gamma^{\pm}_{h}}\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\sigma.

By virtue of (
3.3
), we have

(3.32)

\displaystyle\int_{S_{h}}\mathcal{L}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{w}^{(1,2)})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{S_{h}}(\mathbf{G}_{1}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{G}_{2}^{(1,2)})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}=\int_{S_{h}}(\mathbf{f}_{1}+\mathbf{f}_{2}+\mathbf{f}_{3}+\mathbf{f}_{4})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{S_{h}}(\mathbf{f}_{1j}+\mathbf{f}_{2}+\mathbf{f}_{3}+\mathbf{f}_{4})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}+\int_{S_{h}}(\mathbf{f}_{1}-\mathbf{f}_{1j})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}.

From (
3.30
), (
3.31
) and (
3.32
), we can derive (
3.28
).
∎

Similar to Lemma
2.7
, for the integral
\widetilde{I}_{\Lambda_{h}}
defined in (
3.28
) one has

Lemma 3.5
.

Recall that
\widetilde{I}_{\Lambda_{h}}
is defined in (
3.29
). Under the same setup in Lemma
3.4
, the following integral estimate holds

(3.33)

|\widetilde{I}_{\Lambda_{h}}|\leq C\frac{\sqrt{2h+s^{2}}+\mu s}{\sqrt{2}}\sqrt{\theta_{M}-\theta_{m}}e^{-s\sqrt{h}\delta_{W}}\|\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{w}^{(1,2)}\|_{H^{1}(S_{h})^{2}},

where
C
is a positive constant coming from the trace theorem,
S_{h}
and
\delta_{W}>0
are defined in (
2.2
) and (
2.19
), respectively.

Lemma 3.6
.

Under the same setup in Lemma
3.4
, for any given positive constants
\gamma
,
\beta_{1}
and
\beta_{2}
, we assume that there exits a sequence of the Herglotz wave functions
\{\mathbf{v}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{+\infty}
, where
\mathbf{v}_{j}
is defined by (
3.3
), can approximate
\mathbf{v}
in
H^{1}(S_{h}\times(-M,M))^{3}
satisfying

(3.34)

\|\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{v}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(S_{h}\times(-M,M))^{3}}\leq j^{-\gamma},\ \|\mathbf{g}_{jp}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{3}}\leq j^{\beta_{1}},\ \|\mathbf{g}_{js}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{3}}\leq j^{\beta_{2}}.

Furthermore, suppose that
\eta\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}}\times[-M,M])
,
0<\alpha<1
, and hence it holds that

(3.35)

\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\eta(\mathbf{0})+\delta\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),\ |\delta\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|\leq\|\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\|_{C^{\alpha}}|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{\alpha}

for
0<\alpha<1
.
Recall that
\widetilde{I}_{2}
and
\widetilde{I}_{\pm}^{\Delta}
are defined in (
3.29
). Then we have the following estimates

(3.36)

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{I}_{2}\right|

\displaystyle\leq h\sqrt{2L(\theta_{M}-\theta_{m})}e^{-s\sqrt{\Theta}\delta_{W}}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}j^{-\gamma},

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{I}_{\pm}^{\Delta}\right|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\phi\|_{\infty}(|\eta(0)|h\sqrt{\theta_{M}-\theta_{m}}e^{-s\sqrt{\Theta}\delta_{W}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\frac{2\sqrt{\theta_{M}-\theta_{m}\Gamma(4\alpha+4)}}{(2\delta_{W})^{2\alpha+2}}s^{-2\alpha-2})j^{-\gamma},

where
C
is a positive constants depending on
L
and
h
.

Proof.

Clearly,
\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}
can be approximated by
\{\mathbf{v}_{j}^{\sf R}\}_{j=1}^{+\infty}
in the sense of (
3.34
). Therefore, by the definition of
\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}^{(1,2)}
given by (
3.13
) and
\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}_{j}
given by (
), it can be directly seen that

(3.37)

\|\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)}\|_{H^{1}(S_{h}\times(-L,L))^{2}}\leq\|\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}-\mathbf{v}_{j}^{\sf{R}}\|_{H^{1}(S_{h}\times(-M,M))^{3}}\leq j^{-\gamma}.

By (
3.37
) and using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

(3.38)

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)})-\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}_{j})\|_{L^{2}(S_{h})^{2}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\int_{S_{h}}\left|\int_{-L}^{L}\phi(x_{3})(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3})-\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}_{j}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3})){\rm d}x_{3}\right|^{2}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq 2L\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\mathbf{v}-\mathbf{v}_{j}\|_{L^{2}(S_{h}\times(-L,L))^{3}}^{2}.

By virtue of (
2.20
) and (
3.38
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{I}_{2}\right|=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{S_{h}}(\omega^{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)})-\omega^{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}_{j}))\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\omega^{2}\|{\mathcal{R}}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)})-{\mathcal{R}}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}_{j})\|_{L^{2}(S_{h})^{2}}\|\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\|_{L^{2}(S_{h})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Ch\sqrt{\theta_{M}-\theta_{m}}e^{-s\sqrt{\Theta}\delta_{W}}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}j^{-\gamma}.

Using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the trace theorem, according to (
3.35
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{I}_{\pm}^{\Delta}\right|\leq

\displaystyle|\eta(\mathbf{0})|\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}}|\mathbf{u}||\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}_{j})|{\rm d}\sigma+\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\int_{\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}}|\mathbf{x^{\prime}}|^{\alpha}|\mathbf{u}||\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}_{j})|{\rm d}\sigma

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\eta(\mathbf{0})|\|\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}_{j})\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Gamma_{h}^{\pm})^{2}}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Gamma_{h}^{\pm})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\|\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}_{j})\|_{H^{\frac{1}{2}}(\Gamma_{h}^{\pm})^{2}}\|\||\mathbf{x^{\prime}}|^{\alpha}\mathbf{u}\|_{H^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\Gamma_{h}^{\pm})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\eta(\mathbf{0})|\|\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}_{j})\|_{H^{1}(S_{h})^{2}}\|\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}(S_{h})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\|\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)})\|_{H^{1}(S_{h})^{2}}\|\||\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{\alpha}\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}(S_{h})^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\phi\|_{L^{{\infty}}}\|\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}-\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}_{j}\|_{H^{1}(S_{h})^{2}}(|\eta(\mathbf{0})|\|\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}(S_{h})^{2}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\||\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{\alpha}\mathbf{u}\|_{L^{2}(S_{h})^{2}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}(|\eta(\mathbf{0})|h\sqrt{\theta_{M}-\theta_{m}}e^{-s\sqrt{\Theta}\delta_{W}}+\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\frac{2\sqrt{\theta_{M}-\theta_{m}\Gamma(4\alpha+4)}}{(2\delta_{W})^{2\alpha+2}}s^{-2\alpha-2})j^{-\gamma},

where
C
is a positive constant and the last second inequality comes from Lemma
3.1
.
∎

Lemma 3.7
.

Let
\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})
,
\mathbf{f}_{3}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})
and
\mathbf{f}_{4}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})
be defined in (
3.29
). If

(3.39)

q\mathbf{w}_{\sf{R}}\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S}_{h}\times[-M,M]),

then
\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\in C^{\alpha}(S_{h})
. Furthermore we assume that

(3.40)

\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}-\mathbf{w}_{\sf{R}}\in C^{1,\alpha}(\overline{S}_{h}\times[-M,M])^{3},

where
\alpha\in(0,1)
. Then one has
\mathbf{f}_{3}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S}_{h})
and
\mathbf{f}_{4}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S}_{h})
.

Proof.

According to (
3.40
), one handily computes that

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{f}_{3}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})-\mathbf{f}_{3}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\right|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\lambda|\left|\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime\prime}(x_{3})\left(\begin{pmatrix}(v_{1}-w_{1})\\
(v_{2}-w_{2})\end{pmatrix}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime},x_{3})-\begin{pmatrix}(v_{1}-w_{1})\\
(v_{2}-w_{2})\end{pmatrix}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3})\right){\rm d}x_{3}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2C|\lambda|L\|\phi\|_{C^{2}}\|\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}-\mathbf{w}_{\sf{R}}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(({S}_{h}\times(-M,M))^{3})}\|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\|^{\alpha},

\displaystyle|\mathbf{f}_{4}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})-\mathbf{f}_{4}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\lambda+\mu|\Bigg|\int^{L}_{-L}\phi^{\prime}(x_{3})\Big(\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{1}(v_{3}-w_{3})\\
\partial_{2}(v_{3}-w_{3})\end{pmatrix}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime},x_{3})

\displaystyle-\begin{pmatrix}\partial_{1}(v_{3}-w_{3})\\
\partial_{2}(v_{3}-w_{3})\end{pmatrix}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime},x_{3})\Big){\rm d}x_{3}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2|\lambda+\mu|L\|\phi\|_{C^{1}}\|\mathbf{v}_{\sf{R}}-\mathbf{w}_{\sf{R}}\|_{C^{1,\alpha}(({S}_{h}\times(-M,M))^{3})}\|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}-\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\|^{\alpha},

where
C
is a positive constant. Therefore we know that
\mathbf{f}_{3}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S}_{h})
and
\mathbf{f}_{4}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S}_{h})
.

Due to (
3.39
),
\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S}_{h})
can be obtained directly by virtue of Lemma
3.1
.
∎

Lemma 3.8
.

Recall that
\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})
,
\mathbf{f}_{3}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})
and
\mathbf{f}_{4}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})
are defined in (
3.29
). Suppose that
\mathbf{f}_{\ell}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S}_{h})
(
\ell=2,3,4
),
0<\alpha<1
. Recall that
\widetilde{I}_{1}
is defined in (
3.29
), then the following integral estimates hold

(3.41)

\displaystyle\left|\widetilde{I}_{1}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 6(\mathbf{f}_{1j}(\mathbf{0})+\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{0})+\mathbf{f}_{3}(\mathbf{0})+\mathbf{f}_{4}(\mathbf{0}))|e^{-2\theta_{M}{\rm i}}-e^{-2\theta_{m}\rm i}|s^{-4}

\displaystyle+\sum_{\ell=2}^{4}\|\mathbf{f}_{\ell}\|_{C^{\alpha}(S_{h})^{2}}\frac{2\sqrt{2}(\theta_{M}-\theta_{m})\Gamma(2\alpha+4)}{\delta_{W}^{2\alpha+4}}s^{-2\alpha-4}

\displaystyle+4L\omega^{2}{\rm diam}(S_{h})^{1-\alpha}\sqrt{\pi}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\sum_{\beta=p,\,s}(1+k_{\beta})\bigg(\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},j\beta}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}+\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},j\beta}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}\bigg),

where
\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},j\beta}^{(1,2)}
and
\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},j\beta}^{(1,2)}
are defined in (
3.5
) (
\beta=p,\ s
),
\delta_{W}
is defined in (
2.19
),
\theta_{m}
and
\theta_{M}
are defined in (
1.2
).

Proof.

Due to
\mathbf{f}_{\ell}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S}_{h})
(
\ell=2,3,4
), one has

(3.42)

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{0})+\delta\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),\ |\delta\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|\leq\|\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}(S_{h})^{2}}|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{\alpha},

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{3}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\mathbf{f}_{3}(\mathbf{0})+\delta\mathbf{f}_{3}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),\ |\delta\mathbf{f}_{3}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|\leq\|\mathbf{f}_{3}\|_{C^{\alpha}(S_{h})^{2}}|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{\alpha},

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{4}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\mathbf{f}_{4}(\mathbf{0})+\delta\mathbf{f}_{4}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),\ |\delta\mathbf{f}_{4}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|\leq\|\mathbf{f}_{4}\|_{C^{\alpha}(S_{h})^{2}}|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{\alpha}.

It is easy to see that
\mathbf{f}_{1j}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{S}_{h})
. Hence it yields that

(3.43)

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}_{1j}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=\mathbf{f}_{1j}(\mathbf{0})+\delta\mathbf{f}_{1j}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}),\ |\delta\mathbf{f}_{1j}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|\leq\|\mathbf{f}_{1j}\|_{C^{\alpha}(S_{h})^{2}}|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{\alpha}.

By virtue of (
3.42
) and (
3.43
), we have

(3.44)

\displaystyle\widetilde{I}_{1}=

\displaystyle(\mathbf{f}_{1j}(\mathbf{0})+\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{0})+\mathbf{f}_{3}(\mathbf{0})+\mathbf{f}_{4}(\mathbf{0}))\int_{S_{h}}\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}+\int_{S_{h}}\delta\mathbf{f}_{1j}\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\int_{S_{h}}\delta\mathbf{f}_{2}\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}+\int_{S_{h}}\delta\mathbf{f}_{3}\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}+\int_{S_{h}}\delta\mathbf{f}_{4}\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}.

Recall that
\mathbf{f}_{1j}=-\omega^{2}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)})
. Using the property of compact embedding of Hölder spaces, we can derive that for
0<\alpha<1
,

\|\mathbf{f}_{1j}\|_{C^{\alpha}}\leq\omega^{2}{\rm diam}(S_{h})^{1-\alpha}\|{\mathcal{R}}(\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)})\|_{C^{1}},

where
{\rm diam}(S_{h})
is the diameter of
S_{h}
. By the definition of the dimension reduction operator (
3.1
), using (
3.5
) and (
3.9
), it is easy to see that

\displaystyle|{\mathcal{R}}(\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4L\sqrt{\pi}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigg(\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},jp}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}+\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},jp}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},js}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}+\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},js}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}\bigg),

\displaystyle|\partial_{x_{i}}{\mathcal{R}}(\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4L\sqrt{\pi}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigg(k_{p}\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},jp}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}+k_{p}\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},jp}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}

\displaystyle+k_{s}\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},js}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}+k_{s}\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},js}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}\bigg).

Thus we have

(3.45)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{R}}(\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)})\|_{C^{1}}\leq

\displaystyle 4L\sqrt{\pi}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigg((1+k_{p})\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},jp}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}+(1+k_{p})\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},jp}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}

\displaystyle+(1+k_{s})\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},js}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}+(1+k_{s})\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},js}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}\bigg).

From (
2.18
) and (
3.45
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{S_{h}}\delta\mathbf{f}_{1j}\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\right|\leq\|\mathbf{f}_{1j}\|_{C^{\alpha}(S_{h})^{2}}\left|\int_{W}\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{\alpha}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{f}_{1j}\|_{C^{\alpha}(S_{h})^{2}}\frac{2\sqrt{2}(\theta_{M}-\theta_{m})\Gamma(2\alpha+4)}{\delta_{W}^{2\alpha+4}}s^{-2\alpha-4}.

\displaystyle\leq\omega^{2}{\rm diam}(S_{h})^{1-\alpha}4L\sqrt{\pi}\|\phi\|_{L^{\infty}}\bigg((1+k_{p})\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},jp}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}+(1+k_{p})\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},jp}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}

(3.46)

\displaystyle+(1+k_{s})\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},js}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}+(1+k_{s})\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},js}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}({\mathbb{S}}^{2})}\bigg)s^{-2\alpha-4}.

Similarly, we have

\displaystyle\left|\int_{S_{h}}\delta\mathbf{f}_{2}\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}(S_{h})^{2}}\left|\int_{W}\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{\alpha}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\right|

(3.47)

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{f}_{2}\|_{C^{\alpha}(S_{h})^{2}}\frac{2\sqrt{2}(\theta_{M}-\theta_{m})\Gamma(2\alpha+4)}{\delta_{W}^{2\alpha+4}}s^{-2\alpha-4},

\displaystyle\left|\int_{S_{h}}\delta\mathbf{f}_{3}\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{f}_{3}\|_{C^{\alpha}(S_{h})^{2}}\left|\int_{W}\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{\alpha}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\right|

(3.48)

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{f}_{3}\|_{C^{\alpha}(S_{h})^{2}}\frac{2\sqrt{2}(\theta_{M}-\theta_{m})\Gamma(2\alpha+4)}{\delta_{W}^{2\alpha+4}}s^{-2\alpha-4},

\displaystyle\left|\int_{S_{h}}\delta\mathbf{f}_{4}\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{f}_{4}\|_{C^{\alpha}(S_{h})^{2}}\left|\int_{W}\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{\alpha}{\rm d}\mathbf{x}^{\prime}\right|

(3.49)

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{f}_{4}\|_{C^{\alpha}(S_{h})^{2}}\frac{2\sqrt{2}(\theta_{M}-\theta_{m})\Gamma(2\alpha+4)}{\delta_{W}^{2\alpha+4}}s^{-2\alpha-4}.

From (
3.44
), (
3.46
), (
3.47
), (
3.48
) and (
3.49
), we can derive (
3.41
).
∎

Lemma 3.9
.

Let
j_{\ell}(t)
be the
\ell
-th spherical Bessel function with the form

j_{\ell}(t)=\frac{t^{\ell}}{(2\ell+1)!!}\left(1-\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{l}t^{2l}}{2^{l}l!N_{\ell,l}}\right),

where
N_{\ell,l}=(2\ell+3)\cdots(2\ell+2l+1)
and
\mathcal{R}
be the dimension reduction operator defined in Definition
3.1
. Then

(3.50a)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}(j_{0}(k_{\beta}|\mathbf{x}|))(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=C(\phi)+C_{1}(\phi)\sum^{\infty}_{l=1}\frac{(-1)^{l}k_{\beta}^{2l}(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+a_{0,l}^{2})^{l-\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{l}l!(2l+1)!!}|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2},

(3.50b)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}(j_{2\ell}(k_{\beta}|\mathbf{x}|))(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=C_{1}(\phi)|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}\frac{k_{\beta}^{2\ell}(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+a_{\ell,1}^{2})^{\ell-\frac{1}{2}}}{(4\ell+1)!!}\left[1-\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{l}k_{\beta}^{2l}(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+a_{\ell,l,1}^{2})^{l}}{2^{l}l!N_{\ell,l,1}}\right],

(3.50c)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}(j_{2\ell-1}(k_{\beta}|\mathbf{x}|))(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=C_{1}(\phi)|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}\frac{k_{\beta}^{2\ell-1}(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+a_{\ell,1}^{2})^{\ell-1}}{(4\ell-1)!!}\left[1-\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{(-1)^{l}k_{\beta}^{2l}(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+a_{\ell,l,1}^{2})^{l}}{2^{l}l!N_{\ell,l,2}}\right],

where
\beta=p,s
,
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,
a_{0,l},\xi,a_{\ell,1},\,a_{\ell,l,1}\in(-L,L)
,
N_{\ell,l,1}=(4\ell+3)\cdots(4\ell+2l+1)
,
N_{\ell,l,2}=(4\ell+1)\cdots(4\ell+2l-1)
and

(3.51)

C(\phi)=\int_{-L}^{L}\phi(x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3}>0,\quad C_{1}(\phi)=\int_{-\arctan L/|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|}^{\arctan L/|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|}\phi(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|\tan\omega)\sec^{3}\omega{\rm d}\omega.

Furthermore, it holds that

(3.52)

0<C_{1}(\phi)<\sec^{3}\varpi C(\phi).

where
\varpi\in(-\arctan L/|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|,\arctan L/|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|)
.

Proof.

By virtue of (
3.1
), we have

(3.53)

\displaystyle{\mathcal{R}}(j_{0})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})=

\displaystyle\int^{L}_{-L}\phi(x_{3})\bigg(1-\sum^{\infty}_{l=1}\frac{(-1)^{l}k_{\beta}^{2l}(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+x_{3}^{2})^{l}}{2^{l}l!(2l+1)!!}\bigg){\rm d}x_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int^{L}_{-L}\phi(x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3}+\int^{L}_{-L}\phi(x_{3})\sum^{\infty}_{l=1}\frac{(-1)^{l}k_{\beta}^{2l}(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+x_{3}^{2})^{l}}{2^{l}l!(2l+1)!!}{\rm d}x_{3}.

For
\int^{L}_{-L}\phi(x_{3})\sum^{\infty}_{l=1}\frac{(-1)^{l}k_{\beta}^{2l}(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+x_{3}^{2})^{l}}{2^{l}l!(2l+1)!!}{\rm d}x_{3}
, using integral mean value theorem, we have

(3.54)

\displaystyle\int^{L}_{-L}\phi(x_{3})\sum^{\infty}_{l=1}\frac{(-1)^{l}k_{\beta}^{2l}(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+x_{3}^{2})^{l}}{2^{l}l!(2l+1)!!}{\rm d}x_{3}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum^{\infty}_{l=1}\frac{(-1)^{l}k_{\beta}^{2l}(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+a_{0,l}^{2})^{l-\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{l}l!(2l+1)!!}\int^{L}_{-L}\phi(x_{3})(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+x_{3}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}{\rm d}x_{3}

where
a_{0,l}\in(-L,L)
. By change of variables, where
x_{3}=|x^{\prime}|\tan\omega
, we have

(3.55)

\displaystyle\int^{L}_{-L}\phi(x_{3})(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+x_{3}^{2})^{\frac{1}{2}}{\rm d}x_{3}=\int_{-\arctan\frac{L}{|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|}}^{\arctan\frac{L}{|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|}}\phi(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|\tan\omega)|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}\sec^{3}\omega{\rm d}\omega=|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}C_{1}(\phi).

Plugging (
3.54
) and (
3.55
) into (
3.53
), it yields (
3.50a
).

We proceed to prove (
3.52
). Using the integral mean value theorem and variable substitution
\omega=\arctan\frac{x_{3}}{|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|}
, we have

\displaystyle C_{1}(\phi)=\sec^{3}\varpi\int_{-\arctan L/|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|}^{\arctan L/|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|}\phi(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|\tan\omega){\rm d}\omega=\sec^{3}\varpi\int_{-L}^{L}\phi(x_{3})\frac{|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}}{|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+x_{3}^{2}}{\rm d}x_{3},

where
\varpi\in(-\arctan L/|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|,\arctan L/|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|)
. Using the integral mean value theorem again, we have

\displaystyle C_{1}(\phi)=\frac{|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}}{|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+\xi^{2}}\sec^{3}\varpi\int_{-L}^{L}\phi(x_{3}){\rm d}x_{3}\leq\sec^{3}\varpi C(\phi),

where
\xi\in(-L,L)
.

The series expansion of
{\mathcal{R}}(j_{2\ell})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})
and
{\mathcal{R}}(j_{2\ell-1})(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})
in (
3.50b
) and (
3.50c
) can be obtained by a similar argument. ∎

Remark 3.1
.

We should emphasize that
C_{1}(\phi)
is uniformly bounded with respect to
|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|
since
\varpi\in(-\arctan\frac{L}{|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|},\arctan\frac{L}{|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|})
is fixed, which shall be used in the proof of Theorem
3.1
in what follows.

Lemma 3.10
.

Consider the same setup in Lemma
3.4
and recall that
\widetilde{I}_{\pm}
is defined (
3.29
). Suppose that
\eta\in C^{\alpha}(\overline{\Gamma_{h}^{\pm}}\times[-M,M])
is independent of
x_{3}
and has the expansion (
3.35
), then it holds that

(3.56)

\widetilde{I}_{\pm}=\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{1}+\eta(\mathbf{0})\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{2},

where
\widetilde{I}_{\pm}
are defined in (
3.29
) and

\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{1}=\int_{\Gamma^{\pm}_{h}}\delta\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\sigma,\quad\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{2}=\int_{\Gamma^{\pm}_{h}}\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}_{j}^{(1,2)})\cdot\mathbf{u}(\mathbf{x}^{\prime}){\rm d}\sigma.

Furthermore, the following estimate holds as
s\rightarrow+\infty
:

(3.57)

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{1}\right|\leq

\displaystyle O(s^{-2\alpha-6})+\sum_{\alpha={\sf{R}},{\sf{I}}\atop{\beta=p,s}}\bigg(\|\mathbf{g}_{\alpha,j\beta}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{2}}+\|\mathbf{g}_{\alpha,j\beta}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{2}}\bigg)\times O(s^{-2\alpha-6}).

where
\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},j\beta}^{(1,2)}
and
\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},j\beta}^{(1,2)}
are defined in (
3.5
).

Proof.

One can immediately deduce (
3.56
) by (
3.35
).
By virtue of (
3.27
)
and the reduction operator
\mathcal{R}
defined in (
3.1
), we deduce that

(3.58)

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(\mathbf{v}^{(1,2)}_{j}(\mathbf{x}))

\displaystyle=\mathbf{v}_{jp}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{0})\mathcal{R}(j_{0}(k_{p}|\mathbf{x}|))+\mathbf{v}_{js}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{0})\mathcal{R}(j_{0}(k_{s}|\mathbf{x}|))

\displaystyle+2\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell+1)\mathcal{R}(j_{2\ell}(k_{p}|\mathbf{x}|))\mathbf{B}_{jp,1}^{(\ell)}+2\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell+1)

\displaystyle\times\mathcal{R}(j_{2\ell}(k_{s}|\mathbf{x}|))\mathbf{B}_{js,1}^{(\ell)}+2\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell-1)\mathcal{R}(j_{2\ell-1}(k_{p}|\mathbf{x}|))\mathbf{B}_{jp,2}^{(\ell)}

\displaystyle+2\sum^{+\infty}_{\ell=1}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell-1)\mathcal{R}(j_{2\ell-1}(k_{s}|\mathbf{x}|))\mathbf{B}_{js,2}^{(\ell)}.

Plugging (
3.58
) into
\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{1}
, together with the use of (
2.16
), one can directly verify that

(3.59)

\displaystyle\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{1}=\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{11}+\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{12}+\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{13}+\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{14}+\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{15}+\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{16},

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{11}=\int_{\Gamma^{\pm}_{h}}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
{\rm i}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{jp}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{0})\delta\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\mathcal{R}(j_{0}(k_{p}|\mathbf{x}|))u_{1}{\rm d}\sigma,

\displaystyle\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{12}=\int_{\Gamma^{\pm}_{h}}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
{\rm i}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{js}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{0})\delta\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\mathcal{R}(j_{0}(k_{s}|\mathbf{x}|))u_{1}{\rm d}\sigma,

\displaystyle\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{13}=\sum^{\infty}_{\ell=1}\int_{\Gamma^{\pm}_{h}}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
{\rm i}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\mathbf{B}_{jp,1}^{(\ell)}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell+1)\delta\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\mathcal{R}(j_{2\ell}(k_{p}|\mathbf{x}|))u_{1}{\rm d}\sigma,

\displaystyle\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{14}=\sum^{\infty}_{\ell=1}\int_{\Gamma^{\pm}_{h}}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
{\rm i}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\mathbf{B}_{js,1}^{(\ell)}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell+1)\delta\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\mathcal{R}(j_{2\ell}(k_{s}|\mathbf{x}|))u_{1}{\rm d}\sigma,

\displaystyle\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{15}=\sum^{\infty}_{\ell=1}\int_{\Gamma^{\pm}_{h}}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
{\rm i}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\mathbf{B}_{jp,2}^{(\ell)}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell-1)\delta\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\mathcal{R}(j_{2\ell-1}(k_{p}|\mathbf{x}|))u_{1}{\rm d}\sigma,

\displaystyle\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{16}=\sum^{\infty}_{\ell=1}\int_{\Gamma^{\pm}_{h}}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
{\rm i}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\mathbf{B}_{js,2}^{(\ell)}(-1)^{\ell}(4\ell-1)\delta\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\mathcal{R}(j_{2\ell-1}(k_{s}|\mathbf{x}|))u_{1}{\rm d}\sigma.

By virtue of (
2.48
), (
3.35
), (
3.50a
) and (
3.52
), using the integral mean value theorem, one has as
s\rightarrow+\infty
,

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-}_{11}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|\int_{\Gamma^{-}_{h}}\begin{pmatrix}1\\
{\rm i}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\mathbf{v}_{jp}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{0})\delta\eta(\mathbf{x}^{\prime})\Bigg[C(\phi)+C_{1}(\phi)\sum^{\infty}_{l=1}\frac{(-1)^{l}k_{p}^{2l}(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+a_{0,l}^{2})^{l-\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{l}l!(2l+1)!!}|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}\Bigg]u_{1}{\rm d}\sigma\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}|C(\phi)|\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\left|\mathbf{v}_{jp}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{0})\right|\left|\int_{0}^{h}r^{\alpha}\Bigg[1+\sec^{3}\varpi\sum^{\infty}_{l=1}\frac{(-1)^{l}k_{p}^{2l}(|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}+a_{0,l}^{2})^{l-\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{l}l!(2l+1)!!}|\mathbf{x}^{\prime}|^{2}\Bigg]u_{1}{\rm d}r\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}|C(\phi)|\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\left|\mathbf{v}_{jp}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{0})\right|\int_{0}^{h}r^{\alpha}e^{-s\sqrt{r}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}}{\rm d}r+\sqrt{2}|C(\phi)|\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}

\displaystyle\quad\times\left|\mathbf{v}_{jp}^{(1,2)}(\mathbf{0})\right|\left|\sec^{3}\varpi\sum^{\infty}_{l=1}\frac{(-1)^{l}k_{p}^{2l}(\beta_{0l}^{2}+a_{0,l}^{2})^{l-\frac{1}{2}}}{2^{l}l!(2l+1)!!}\right|\int_{0}^{h}r^{\alpha+2}e^{-s\sqrt{r}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}}{\rm d}r

(3.60)

\displaystyle=O(s^{-2\alpha-6}),

where
\beta_{0l}\in(0,h)
and
a_{0,l}\in(-L,L)
. Similarly, we can derive that

(3.61)

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{+}_{11}\right|\leq O(s^{-2\alpha-6}),\quad\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{12}\right|\leq O(s^{-2\alpha-6})\quad\mbox{as $s\rightarrow+\infty$.}

From (
3.9
), using Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we have

(3.62)

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{B}_{jp,1}^{(\ell)}\right|\leq 2\sqrt{\pi}\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},jp}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{2}},\ \left|\mathbf{B}_{js,1}^{(\ell)}\right|\leq 2\sqrt{\pi}\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},js}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{2}},

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{B}_{jp,2}^{(\ell)}\right|\leq 2\sqrt{\pi}\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},jp}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{2}},\ \left|\mathbf{B}_{js,2}^{(\ell)}\right|\leq 2\sqrt{\pi}\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},js}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{2}}.

From (
2.48
), (
3.50a
) and (
3.62
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-}_{13}\right|

\displaystyle\leq|\sec^{3}\varpi|\left|C(\phi)\right|\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\sum^{\infty}_{\ell=1}\left|4\ell+1\right|\left|\begin{pmatrix}1\\
{\rm i}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\mathbf{B}_{jp,1}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\times\left|\int_{0}^{h}r^{\alpha+2}{\frac{k^{2\ell}_{p}(r^{2}+a_{\ell}^{2})^{\ell-\frac{1}{2}}}{(4\ell+1)!!}\Bigg[1-\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{k_{p}^{2l}(r^{2}+a_{\ell,l}^{2})^{l}}{2^{l}l!N_{\ell,l,1}}\Bigg]e^{-s\sqrt{r}\mu(\theta_{m})}}{\rm d}r\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2}|\sec^{3}\varpi|\left|C(\phi)\right|\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\sum^{\infty}_{\ell=1}\left|4\ell+1\right|\left|\mathbf{B}_{jp,1}\right|

\displaystyle\quad\times\frac{k^{2\ell}_{p}(\beta_{\ell}^{2}+\alpha_{\ell}^{2})^{\ell-\frac{1}{2}}}{(2\ell+1)!!}\left|1-\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{k_{p}^{2l}(\beta_{\ell,l}^{2}+\alpha_{\ell,l}^{2})^{l}}{2^{l}l!N_{\ell,l}}\right|\int_{0}^{h}r^{\alpha+2}e^{-s\sqrt{r}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}}{\rm d}r

(3.63)

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},jp}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{2}}\times O(s^{-2\alpha-6}),

as
s\rightarrow+\infty
,
where
\beta_{\ell l},\,\beta_{\ell}\in(0,h)
and
a_{\ell,l}\in(-L,L)
. Similarly, we can derive that

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{+}_{13}\right|\leq\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},jp}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{2}}\times O(s^{-2\alpha-6}),\quad\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{14}\right|\leq\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{R}},js}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{2}}\times O(s^{-2\alpha-6}),

as
s\rightarrow+\infty
.
By (

) and (
3.50a
), we have

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{-}_{15}\right|\leq

\displaystyle|C(\phi)|\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\sum^{\infty}_{\ell=1}\left|4\ell-1\right|\left|\begin{pmatrix}1\\
{\rm i}\end{pmatrix}\cdot\mathbf{B}_{jp,2}^{(\ell)}\right|

\displaystyle\times\left|\int_{0}^{h}r^{\alpha}{\frac{k^{2\ell-1}_{p}(r^{2}+a_{\ell}^{2})^{\ell-1}}{(4\ell-1)!!}\Bigg[1-\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{k^{2l}_{p}(r^{2}+a_{\ell,l}^{2})^{l}}{2^{l}l!N_{\ell,l,1}}\Bigg]e^{-s\sqrt{r}\mu(\theta_{m})}}{\rm d}r\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2}|C(\phi)|\|\eta\|_{C^{\alpha}}\sum^{\infty}_{\ell=1}\left|4\ell-1\right|\left|\mathbf{B}_{jp,2}^{(\ell)}\right|

\displaystyle\times\frac{k^{2\ell-1}_{p}(\beta_{\ell}^{2}+\alpha_{\ell}^{2})^{\ell-1}}{(2\ell+1)!!}\left|1-\sum_{l=1}^{\infty}\frac{k^{2l}_{p}(\beta_{\ell,l}^{2}+a_{\ell,l}^{2})^{l}}{2^{l}l!N_{\ell,l}}\right|\int_{0}^{h}r^{\alpha+2}e^{-s\sqrt{r}\cos\frac{\theta}{2}}{\rm d}r

(3.65)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},jp}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{2}}\times O(s^{-2\alpha-6}),

as
s\rightarrow+\infty
,
where
\beta_{\ell l},\,\beta_{\ell}\in(0,h)
and
a_{\ell,l}\in(-L,L)
. Similarly, we can derive that

(3.66)

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{+}_{15}\right|\leq\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},jp}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{2}}\times O(s^{-2\alpha-6}),\quad\left|\mathcal{\widetilde{I}}^{\pm}_{16}\right|\leq\|\mathbf{g}_{{\sf{I}},js}^{(1,2)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})^{2}}\times O(s^{-2\alpha-6}),

From (

), (

), (

), (

), (
3.65
) and (
3.66

Lemma 3.13
.

Consider the same setup in Lemma
3.2
and suppose that

can be approximated by a sequence of the functions

). Since (

3.3
). According to (

), one has

By virtue of (

and

, adopting a similar argument for
